========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, distress... I mean, I still get my work done. Mr. Henderson hasn't complained, though honestly, he'd complain if the sky was too blue, so who knows. But I have noticed the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning... you know, trying to make sure I look okay before I leave... it's made me rush into the courthouse a few mornings. 

And parking at the courthouse! Don't even get me started. They've been doing construction on 4th Street for what feels like a decade. They just leave those orange cones out, and half the time there aren't even any workers there! Just cones, blocking a perfectly good lane of traffic. Last Thursday, I had to park three blocks away in this garage that charges twenty dollars an hour. Twenty dollars! And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. 

But anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. The instructor, Chloe—she's one of those people who is just aggressively calm, you know? Like, she speaks in this whispery voice that makes me want to scream. She gave me this look when I walked in late on Wednesday. 

And, oh, I've also skipped a few casual photos at my book club. We were reading that new historical fiction novel—the one about the sisters in Paris during the war? Honestly, I thought it was a bit cliché. How many books about Paris in the 1940s do we really need? But Brenda insisted we take a group picture for the neighborhood Facebook page, and I just... I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. Brenda always uses the flash, even in broad daylight. It's so unflattering. So I guess, yeah, it affects things a little bit.

And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, avoiding things... like I said, I still get my work done. I haven't missed any days at the firm, though Mr. Henderson's mood swings make me want to call in sick sometimes. He actually yelled at the copier repair guy yesterday. The repair guy! Like it's his fault the paper tray always jams. We've had that same Xerox machine since, I swear, 2015. It makes this horrible grinding noise every time you print double-sided. I keep telling the office manager, Linda, that we just need to lease a new one, but Linda is so cheap. She buys that single-ply toilet paper for the employee restroom. It's practically transparent! 

But, um, avoiding things... I guess, like I mentioned before, the extra time I spend getting ready... making sure my skin looks okay... it has made me rush into the courthouse a few times. And I've missed the start of Chloe's yoga class more than once. Which, honestly, missing the first five minutes of her deep-breathing exercises isn't the worst thing in the world. She always burns this incense that smells like old socks and lavender. I don't know where she buys it, but it gives me a sinus headache. 

And then there's the book club photos. I told you about Brenda and her terrible flash photography. I've skipped out on a few of those casual pictures, and also at some of the community events our neighborhood association throws. They had this summer block party last month, and they hired a caricature artist. A caricature artist! Why would anyone want their flaws exaggerated on a giant piece of paper? I just made sure to stay by the snack table the whole time. The potato salad was sitting out in the sun way too long anyway, so I had to keep an eye on it so no one got food poisoning. So, yeah, I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in any of those situations.

I mean, I still get my work done. Mr. Henderson hasn't complained, though honestly, he'd complain if the sky was too blue, so who knows. But I have noticed the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning... you know, trying to make sure I look okay before I leave... it's made me rush into the courthouse a few mornings. \n\nAnd parking at the courthouse! Don't even get me started. They've been doing construction on 4th Street for what feels like a decade. They just leave those orange cones out, and half the time there aren't even any workers there! Just cones, blocking a perfectly good lane of traffic. Last Thursday, I had to park three blocks away in this garage that charges twenty dollars an hour. Twenty dollars! And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. The instructor, Chloe—she's one of those people who is just aggressively calm, you know? Like, she speaks in this whispery voice that makes me want to scream. She gave me this look when I walked in late on Wednesday. \n\nAnd, oh, I've also skipped a few casual photos at my book club. We were reading that new historical fiction novel—the one about the sisters in Paris during the war? Honestly, I thought it was a bit cliché. I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. I keep telling the office manager, Linda, that we just need to lease a new one, but Linda is so cheap. She buys that single-ply toilet paper for the employee restroom. It's practically transparent! \n\nBut, um, avoiding things... I guess, like I mentioned before, the extra time I spend getting ready... making sure my skin looks okay... it has made me rush into the courthouse a few times. She always burns this incense that smells like old socks and lavender. I don't know where she buys it, but it gives me a sinus headache. \n\nAnd then there's the book club photos. I just made sure to stay by the snack table the whole time. The potato salad was sitting out in the sun way too long anyway, so I had to keep an eye on it so no one got food poisoning. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, other people... I mean, my husband says he can’t really see what I’m talking about. But I keep noticing it, you know? The skin around my cheeks and jaw just looks so uneven and kind of saggy to me. It really started because we put in that brighter bathroom mirror recently. 

Which, by the way, getting that mirror installed was a complete nightmare. We hired this contractor, Gary, who my sister-in-law recommended. Never use family recommendations, I'm telling you. He said it would take one afternoon, and it took him a week and a half! Just for a mirror and some vanity lights! And he kept leaving his giant metal thermos right on my nice quartz countertop without a coaster. Who does that? I had to follow him around with a microfiber cloth the whole time he was in my house. I swear he was tracking mud in, too, but he denied it. 

But anyway... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, since that really bright mirror went up, I'm just always seeing the sagging and the unevenness, even if my husband swears he doesn't notice it at all. So I don't know if anyone else really sees it or not.

I'm a paralegal at a firm downtown, and honestly, the mornings are always such a rush anyway. We do a lot of personal injury cases, and my boss, Mr. Henderson, he is just... well, he expects everything done yesterday. \n\nJust this past Tuesday he dropped this massive stack of interrogatories on my desk at 4:45 PM. 4:45! And he wanted them formatted and mailed out before I left. I ended up missing my usual train, and the later train is always packed with high school kids being loud and playing music on their phones without headphones, which just gives me a horrible headache. Plus the seats on that train are always so sticky. I don't know why the city doesn't clean them more often with the taxes we pay. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, the time. Yeah, I don't know exactly how long I spend thinking about my skin. I just know it bothers me when I see it in that new light.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a rough estimate of how much time you spend each day thinking about the skin on your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: A rough estimate? I mean... I really couldn't say. It's so hard to keep track of time, honestly. Like, just this past weekend, I thought I was only going to be at the garden center for twenty minutes. I just needed to pick up some mulch for the front flower beds because our neighborhood association sent out one of those passive-aggressive newsletters about curb appeal. Can you believe that? We pay HOA fees every single month, and they have the nerve to complain about a few weeds near our mailbox. \n\nAnyway, I was there, and I ran into my neighbor, Susan, while I was looking at the petunias. She just got a new golden retriever puppy, which is cute, I guess, but she lets it walk all over everyone's lawns. And she kept me talking for what felt like an hour about her daughter's wedding planning. They're trying to book that country club over on Westheimer, but the waitlist is apparently two years long. Two years! Who plans a wedding two years out? By the time they actually get married, whatever bridesmaids' dresses they pick will be completely out of style. I told her she should just look at the botanical gardens, they have a lovely gazebo, but she wouldn't even listen to me. \n\n...Wait, I'm sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, the time. Yeah, I really don't have a number for you. I just don't know. I just notice my skin when I'm at the sink and then I go on with my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your concern about the skin on your cheeks and jaw causes you significant distress or affects your daily life, such as how you interact with others or how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, distress... I mean, I still get my work done. Mr. Henderson hasn't complained, though honestly, he'd complain if the sky was too blue, so who knows. But I have noticed the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning... you know, trying to make sure I look okay before I leave... it's made me rush into the courthouse a few mornings. \n\nAnd parking at the courthouse! Don't even get me started. They've been doing construction on 4th Street for what feels like a decade. They just leave those orange cones out, and half the time there aren't even any workers there! Just cones, blocking a perfectly good lane of traffic. Last Thursday, I had to park three blocks away in this garage that charges twenty dollars an hour. Twenty dollars! And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. The instructor, Chloe—she's one of those people who is just aggressively calm, you know? Like, she speaks in this whispery voice that makes me want to scream. She gave me this look when I walked in late on Wednesday. \n\nAnd, oh, I've also skipped a few casual photos at my book club. We were reading that new historical fiction novel—the one about the sisters in Paris during the war? Honestly, I thought it was a bit cliché. How many books about Paris in the 1940s do we really need? But Brenda insisted we take a group picture for the neighborhood Facebook page, and I just... I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. Brenda always uses the flash, even in broad daylight. It's so unflattering. I just made sure to stay by the snack table the whole time. The potato salad was sitting out in the sun way too long anyway, so I had to keep an eye on it so no one got food poisoning. Well, intrusive... I guess I think about it a fair amount, especially in the mornings when I'm getting ready. But unwanted? I don't know, it's just my face, so I have to think about it. I wouldn't say it's out of my control or anything. I can still focus on work.

But anyway, no, I don't think the thoughts are... what did you call it? Intrusive? I just notice the sagging when I look in the mirror, and then I go about my day. It's not like it's taking over my brain or anything.

Don't even get me started. They've been doing construction on 4th Street for what feels like a decade. They just leave those orange cones out, and half the time there aren't even any workers there! Just cones, blocking a perfectly good lane of traffic. Last Thursday, I had to park three blocks away in this garage that charges twenty dollars an hour. Twenty dollars! And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. The instructor, Chloe—she's one of those people who is just aggressively calm, you know? Like, she speaks in this whispery voice that makes me want to scream. She gave me this look when I walked in late on Wednesday. \n\nAnd, oh, I've also skipped a few casual photos at my book club. We were reading that new historical fiction novel—the one about the sisters in Paris during the war? Honestly, I thought it was a bit cliché. How many books about Paris in the 1940s do we really need? But Brenda insisted we take a group picture for the neighborhood Facebook page, and I just... I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. Brenda always uses the flash, even in broad daylight. It's so unflattering. It's practically transparent! \n\nBut, um, avoiding things... I guess, like I mentioned before, the extra time I spend getting ready... making sure my skin looks okay... it has made me rush into the courthouse a few times. And I've missed the start of Chloe's yoga class more than once. Which, honestly, missing the first five minutes of her deep-breathing exercises isn't the worst thing in the world. She always burns this incense that smells like old socks and lavender. I don't know where she buys it, but it gives me a sinus headache. \n\nAnd then there's the book club photos. I told you about Brenda and her terrible flash photography. I've skipped out on a few of those casual pictures, and also at some of the community events our neighborhood association throws. I had to follow him around with a microfiber cloth the whole time he was in my house. I swear he was tracking mud in, too, but he denied it. \n\nBut anyway... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, since that really bright mirror went up, I'm just always seeing the sagging and the unevenness, even if my husband swears he doesn't notice it at all. He went to some seminar in Chicago last weekend and came back convinced we need to color-code everything. Color-code! Do you know how much time it takes to relabel hundreds of client files? And he wants it done by Friday. I told Linda we need to order more green folders, because we always run out of green, and she just sighed at me. She acts like I'm asking for her personal bank account numbers when I request office supplies. It's not like it's taking over my brain or anything.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that your concern about your skin causes you a level of distress that interferes with your ability to enjoy your life or function the way you’d like?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / paralegal_or_legal_assistant / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"I still get my work done, but the extra time I spend getting ready has made me rush into the courthouse a few mornings and miss the start of yoga more than once. I’ve also skipped a few casual photos at book club and community events because I didn’t want to worry about how my face would look.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, distress... I mean, like I was kind of saying earlier, I still get all my work done. I'm not falling apart or anything. But I guess it does interfere a little, because of that extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings. It’s made me rush into the courthouse a few times. Which is so stressful because the parking at the downtown courthouse is an absolute nightmare. If you aren't in the lot by 8:15, you have to park in the 4th Street garage, and they charge twelve dollars an hour! Twelve dollars! And the elevator in that garage always smells like stale beer. I complained to the parking attendant once—this teenager who didn't even look up from his phone—and he just shrugged at me. Kids today have zero work ethic. My nephew is the same way, he just plays video games in my brother's basement all day... 

But, um, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying my life and functioning. Yeah, so like I mentioned before, I've missed the start of yoga more than once, and I've skipped those casual photos at book club and the community events. I just really didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the pictures, so I just sit them out. Which is fine, I guess, but... speaking of book club, we're reading this new mystery novel right now, and the plot holes are just massive. The main character is a detective, but she goes to confront the killer and leaves her cell phone in her car? Who does that? Brenda loved the book, obviously, but Brenda also thinks mayonnaise is a spice. So I don't really trust her judgment on anything, let alone literature. But yeah, I guess the skin stuff does get in the way of a few things here and there.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, abnormal... I don't know if I'd use the word disfigured, that sounds a bit extreme. But unattractive? Yes, definitely. Like I said before, ever since we put in that brighter bathroom mirror, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks so uneven and kind of saggy to me. It's just right there, staring back at me. Even though my husband says he can’t really see what I’m talking about. But he also didn't notice when I painted the entire guest bedroom a completely different color. I went from "eggshell white" to "seafoam green," which is a very distinct color, let me tell you. I spent a whole weekend taping off the baseboards and moving all the furniture myself. 

And the paint fumes! Oh my goodness, I had to open every window on the second floor, and of course, that was the weekend the neighbor decided to burn leaves in his backyard. Who burns leaves in the city limits anymore? I'm pretty sure there's an ordinance against it. I should know, working at the firm, I read through municipal codes all the time. I almost marched over there and quoted section 4-B to him, but I didn't want to smell like smoke. I already had to wash my hair twice just to get the paint smell out... 

But anyway, what was the question? Oh, right, the skin. Yeah, I just think it looks flawed, you know? Saggy and uneven. It's just really noticeable to me now with that new lighting.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, repeatedly... I mean, I guess I do check it a bit. Most mornings I end up looking at my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, just to see how the sagging looks, and I usually put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. I have to use this special foundation now, it's supposed to be "illuminating," but honestly it just smells like sunscreen. I bought it at that department store downtown, the one that's closing next month. It’s such a shame they’re closing, they always had the best perfume counters. The ladies there would give you those little free samples in the tiny glass vials. I used to keep them in my purse, but then one broke and my entire bag smelled like lavender and sandalwood for six months. Every time I opened it to get my wallet, it was like walking into a spa. My husband hated it, he said it gave him a headache, but he gets a headache if the TV is too loud, so... 

Oh, right, fixing my skin. So yeah, I do the makeup, and sometimes when I'm at work I catch myself comparing my face to the other women in the office. Like Linda, she's older than me but her jawline is so sharp. I don't know how she does it. Probably good genes, or maybe she gets something done, she never says. And like I mentioned before, I do try to avoid being in casual pictures, like at book club or whatever, if I’m not feeling okay about how my face looks that day. But I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or touching my face all day long or anything. Just the mirror checking and the makeup in the morning, mostly.

I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. Brenda always uses the flash, even in broad daylight. It's so unflattering. And he wants it done by Friday. I told Linda we need to order more green folders, because we always run out of green, and she just sighed at me. She acts like I'm asking for her personal bank account numbers when I request office supplies. I mean, it's a law firm, Linda, we need folders! \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think the thoughts are... what did you call it? Intrusive? But he also didn't notice when I painted the entire guest bedroom a completely different color. I went from \"eggshell white\" to \"seafoam green,\" which is a very distinct color, let me tell you. I spent a whole weekend taping off the baseboards and moving all the furniture myself. \n\nAnd the paint fumes! Oh my goodness, I had to open every window on the second floor, and of course, that was the weekend the neighbor decided to burn leaves in his backyard. Who burns leaves in the city limits anymore? I'm pretty sure there's an ordinance against it. I should know, working at the firm, I read through municipal codes all the time. I almost marched over there and quoted section 4-B to him, but I didn't want to smell like smoke. I already had to wash my hair twice just to get the paint smell out... \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Oh, right, the skin. Yeah, I just think it looks flawed, you know? Saggy and uneven. I mean, it's a law firm, Linda, we need folders! \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think the thoughts are... what did you call it? Intrusive? I just notice the sagging when I look in the mirror, and then I go about my day. It's not like it's taking over my brain or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your skin causes you a level of distress that interferes with your ability to enjoy your life or function the way you’d like?\n\nPatient: Well, distress... I mean, like I was kind of saying earlier, I still get all my work done. I'm not falling apart or anything. But I guess it does interfere a little, because of that extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings. It’s made me rush into the courthouse a few times. Which is so stressful because the parking at the downtown courthouse is an absolute nightmare. If you aren't in the lot by 8:15, you have to park in the 4th Street garage, and they charge twelve dollars an hour! Twelve dollars! And the elevator in that garage always smells like stale beer. I complained to the parking attendant once—this teenager who didn't even look up from his phone—and he just shrugged at me. Kids today have zero work ethic. My nephew is the same way, he just plays video games in my brother's basement all day... \n\nBut, um, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying my life and functioning. Yeah, so like I mentioned before, I've missed the start of yoga more than once, and I've skipped those casual photos at book club and the community events. I just really didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the pictures, so I just sit them out. Which is fine, I guess, but... speaking of book club, we're reading this new mystery novel right now, and the plot holes are just massive. The main character is a detective, but she goes to confront the killer and leaves her cell phone in her car? Who does that? But yeah, I guess the skin stuff does get in the way of a few things here and there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the skin on your cheeks and jaw looks physically abnormal, like there's a defect or flaw that makes you appear disfigured or unattractive?\n\nPatient: Well, abnormal... I don't know if I'd use the word disfigured, that sounds a bit extreme. But unattractive? Yes, definitely. Like I said before, ever since we put in that brighter bathroom mirror, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks so uneven and kind of saggy to me. It's just right there, staring back at me. Even though my husband says he can’t really see what I’m talking about. But he also didn't notice when I painted the entire guest bedroom a completely different color. I went from \"eggshell white\" to \"seafoam green,\" which is a very distinct color, let me tell you. I almost marched over there and quoted section 4-B to him, but I didn't want to smell like smoke. I already had to wash my hair twice just to get the paint smell out... \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Oh, right, the skin. Yeah, I just think it looks flawed, you know? Saggy and uneven. It's just really noticeable to me now with that new lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly, like checking mirrors, touching your face, or trying to fix how your skin looks in response to how you feel about it?\n\nPatient: Well, repeatedly... I mean, I guess I do check it a bit. Most mornings I end up looking at my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, just to see how the sagging looks, and I usually put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. I have to use this special foundation now, it's supposed to be \"illuminating,\" but honestly it just smells like sunscreen. I bought it at that department store downtown, the one that's closing next month. It’s such a shame they’re closing, they always had the best perfume counters. The ladies there would give you those little free samples in the tiny glass vials. I used to keep them in my purse, but then one broke and my entire bag smelled like lavender and sandalwood for six months. Every time I opened it to get my wallet, it was like walking into a spa. My husband hated it, he said it gave him a headache, but he gets a headache if the TV is too loud, so... \n\nOh, right, fixing my skin. So yeah, I do the makeup, and sometimes when I'm at work I catch myself comparing my face to the other women in the office. Like Linda, she's older than me but her jawline is so sharp. I don't know how she does it. Probably good genes, or maybe she gets something done, she never says. And like I mentioned before, I do try to avoid being in casual pictures, like at book club or whatever, if I’m not feeling okay about how my face looks that day. Well, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't have to do it so much. Like I was saying, most mornings I end up checking my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, and then I put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. It just takes time, you know? Time I could use to actually enjoy my coffee instead of gulping it down. I buy this really nice French roast from a local roaster, it's fair trade and everything. The guy who runs the shop is so sweet, he always remembers my order. He told me he used to be a jazz musician in New Orleans before he moved up here to roast coffee. Can you imagine? Going from playing the saxophone in the French Quarter to bagging beans in the Midwest. He said he just wanted a quieter life. Which I totally get. Sometimes the noise in the city just drives me up the wall, especially when the garbage trucks come clanging down the alley at 5 AM. I've called the city council about it, but they just transfer you from one department to another until you give up. Typical bureaucracy. 

But, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, the skin stuff and wishing I didn't do it. Yeah, so the makeup and the checking in the morning, and then like I mentioned, sometimes I compare my face to the women I see at work, like Linda. And I do avoid being in those casual pictures at book club or whatever if I’m not feeling okay about how I look. It would be nice to just not care, to just snap a photo and not worry about the sagging or whatever. So, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't feel like I had to do all that.

But yeah, the mirror just really highlights it, and it's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you find yourself thinking about the skin on your cheeks and jaw each day?\n\nPatient: Gosh, how much time... I really couldn't give you an exact number. I haven't exactly timed it or anything. I mean, I notice it in the morning when I'm getting ready for work. I'm a paralegal at a firm downtown, and honestly, the mornings are always such a rush anyway. We do a lot of personal injury cases, and my boss, Mr. Henderson, he is just... well, he expects everything done yesterday. \n\nJust this past Tuesday he dropped this massive stack of interrogatories on my desk at 4:45 PM. 4:45! I ended up missing my usual train, and the later train is always packed with high school kids being loud and playing music on their phones without headphones, which just gives me a horrible headache. Plus the seats on that train are always so sticky. I don't know why the city doesn't clean them more often with the taxes we pay. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, the time. Yeah, I don't know exactly how long I spend thinking about my skin. I just know it bothers me when I see it in that new light.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a rough estimate of how much time you spend each day thinking about the skin on your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: A rough estimate? I mean... I really couldn't say. It's so hard to keep track of time, honestly. Like, just this past weekend, I thought I was only going to be at the garden center for twenty minutes. I just needed to pick up some mulch for the front flower beds because our neighborhood association sent out one of those passive-aggressive newsletters about curb appeal. Can you believe that? We pay HOA fees every single month, and they have the nerve to complain about a few weeds near our mailbox. \n\nAnyway, I was there, and I ran into my neighbor, Susan, while I was looking at the petunias. She just got a new golden retriever puppy, which is cute, I guess, but she lets it walk all over everyone's lawns. And she kept me talking for what felt like an hour about her daughter's wedding planning. They're trying to book that country club over on Westheimer, but the waitlist is apparently two years long. Two years! Who plans a wedding two years out? By the time they actually get married, whatever bridesmaids' dresses they pick will be completely out of style. I told her she should just look at the botanical gardens, they have a lovely gazebo, but she wouldn't even listen to me. \n\n...Wait, I'm sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, the time. Yeah, I really don't have a number for you. I just don't know. I just notice my skin when I'm at the sink and then I go on with my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your concern about the skin on your cheeks and jaw causes you significant distress or affects your daily life, such as how you interact with others or how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, distress... I mean, I still get my work done. Mr. Henderson hasn't complained, though honestly, he'd complain if the sky was too blue, so who knows. But I have noticed the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning... you know, trying to make sure I look okay before I leave... it's made me rush into the courthouse a few mornings. \n\nAnd parking at the courthouse! Don't even get me started. They've been doing construction on 4th Street for what feels like a decade. They just leave those orange cones out, and half the time there aren't even any workers there! Just cones, blocking a perfectly good lane of traffic. Last Thursday, I had to park three blocks away in this garage that charges twenty dollars an hour. Twenty dollars! And the elevator smelled like stale French fries. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I've been rushing. I've even missed the start of my yoga class more than once. The instructor, Chloe—she's one of those people who is just aggressively calm, you know? Like, she speaks in this whispery voice that makes me want to scream. She gave me this look when I walked in late on Wednesday. \n\nAnd, oh, I've also skipped a few casual photos at my book club. We were reading that new historical fiction novel—the one about the sisters in Paris during the war? Honestly, I thought it was a bit cliché. How many books about Paris in the 1940s do we really need? But Brenda insisted we take a group picture for the neighborhood Facebook page, and I just... I kind of slipped out to the kitchen to get more crackers. I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the photo, especially with the flash. Brenda always uses the flash, even in broad daylight. It's so unflattering. So I guess, yeah, it affects things a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any social situations, activities, or events because you're worried about how your face or skin might look?\n\nPatient: Well, avoiding things... like I said, I still get my work done. I haven't missed any days at the firm, though Mr. Henderson's mood swings make me want to call in sick sometimes. He actually yelled at the copier repair guy yesterday. The repair guy! Like it's his fault the paper tray always jams. We've had that same Xerox machine since, I swear, 2015. It makes this horrible grinding noise every time you print double-sided. I keep telling the office manager, Linda, that we just need to lease a new one, but Linda is so cheap. She buys that single-ply toilet paper for the employee restroom. It's practically transparent! \n\nBut, um, avoiding things... I guess, like I mentioned before, the extra time I spend getting ready... making sure my skin looks okay... it has made me rush into the courthouse a few times. And I've missed the start of Chloe's yoga class more than once. Which, honestly, missing the first five minutes of her deep-breathing exercises isn't the worst thing in the world. She always burns this incense that smells like old socks and lavender. I don't know where she buys it, but it gives me a sinus headache. \n\nAnd then there's the book club photos. I told you about Brenda and her terrible flash photography. I've skipped out on a few of those casual pictures, and also at some of the community events our neighborhood association throws. They had this summer block party last month, and they hired a caricature artist. A caricature artist! Why would anyone want their flaws exaggerated on a giant piece of paper? I just made sure to stay by the snack table the whole time. The potato salad was sitting out in the sun way too long anyway, so I had to keep an eye on it so no one got food poisoning. So, yeah, I just didn't want to worry about how my face would look in any of those situations.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people notice or pay attention to the skin on your cheeks and jaw the way you do?\n\nPatient: Well, other people... I mean, my husband says he can’t really see what I’m talking about. But I keep noticing it, you know? The skin around my cheeks and jaw just looks so uneven and kind of saggy to me. It really started because we put in that brighter bathroom mirror recently. \n\nWhich, by the way, getting that mirror installed was a complete nightmare. We hired this contractor, Gary, who my sister-in-law recommended. Never use family recommendations, I'm telling you. He said it would take one afternoon, and it took him a week and a half! Just for a mirror and some vanity lights! And he kept leaving his giant metal thermos right on my nice quartz countertop without a coaster. Who does that? I had to follow him around with a microfiber cloth the whole time he was in my house. I swear he was tracking mud in, too, but he denied it. \n\nBut anyway... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, since that really bright mirror went up, I'm just always seeing the sagging and the unevenness, even if my husband swears he doesn't notice it at all. So I don't know if anyone else really sees it or not.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your skin on your cheeks and jaw, does it feel like your thoughts about it are intrusive or unwanted, and do they feel difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Well, intrusive... I guess I think about it a fair amount, especially in the mornings when I'm getting ready. But unwanted? I don't know, it's just my face, so I have to think about it. I wouldn't say it's out of my control or anything. I can still focus on work. \n\nSpeaking of work, did I mention Mr. Henderson is making us switch to a new filing system? After twenty years! He went to some seminar in Chicago last weekend and came back convinced we need to color-code everything. Color-code! Do you know how much time it takes to relabel hundreds of client files? And he wants it done by Friday. I told Linda we need to order more green folders, because we always run out of green, and she just sighed at me. She acts like I'm asking for her personal bank account numbers when I request office supplies. I mean, it's a law firm, Linda, we need folders! \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think the thoughts are... what did you call it? Intrusive? I just notice the sagging when I look in the mirror, and then I go about my day. It's not like it's taking over my brain or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your skin causes you a level of distress that interferes with your ability to enjoy your life or function the way you’d like?\n\nPatient: Well, distress... I mean, like I was kind of saying earlier, I still get all my work done. I'm not falling apart or anything. But I guess it does interfere a little, because of that extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings. It’s made me rush into the courthouse a few times. Which is so stressful because the parking at the downtown courthouse is an absolute nightmare. If you aren't in the lot by 8:15, you have to park in the 4th Street garage, and they charge twelve dollars an hour! Twelve dollars! And the elevator in that garage always smells like stale beer. I complained to the parking attendant once—this teenager who didn't even look up from his phone—and he just shrugged at me. Kids today have zero work ethic. My nephew is the same way, he just plays video games in my brother's basement all day... \n\nBut, um, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying my life and functioning. Yeah, so like I mentioned before, I've missed the start of yoga more than once, and I've skipped those casual photos at book club and the community events. I just really didn't want to worry about how my face would look in the pictures, so I just sit them out. Which is fine, I guess, but... speaking of book club, we're reading this new mystery novel right now, and the plot holes are just massive. The main character is a detective, but she goes to confront the killer and leaves her cell phone in her car? Who does that? Brenda loved the book, obviously, but Brenda also thinks mayonnaise is a spice. So I don't really trust her judgment on anything, let alone literature. But yeah, I guess the skin stuff does get in the way of a few things here and there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the skin on your cheeks and jaw looks physically abnormal, like there's a defect or flaw that makes you appear disfigured or unattractive?\n\nPatient: Well, abnormal... I don't know if I'd use the word disfigured, that sounds a bit extreme. But unattractive? Yes, definitely. Like I said before, ever since we put in that brighter bathroom mirror, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks so uneven and kind of saggy to me. It's just right there, staring back at me. Even though my husband says he can’t really see what I’m talking about. But he also didn't notice when I painted the entire guest bedroom a completely different color. I went from \"eggshell white\" to \"seafoam green,\" which is a very distinct color, let me tell you. I spent a whole weekend taping off the baseboards and moving all the furniture myself. \n\nAnd the paint fumes! Oh my goodness, I had to open every window on the second floor, and of course, that was the weekend the neighbor decided to burn leaves in his backyard. Who burns leaves in the city limits anymore? I'm pretty sure there's an ordinance against it. I should know, working at the firm, I read through municipal codes all the time. I almost marched over there and quoted section 4-B to him, but I didn't want to smell like smoke. I already had to wash my hair twice just to get the paint smell out... \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Oh, right, the skin. Yeah, I just think it looks flawed, you know? Saggy and uneven. It's just really noticeable to me now with that new lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly, like checking mirrors, touching your face, or trying to fix how your skin looks in response to how you feel about it?\n\nPatient: Well, repeatedly... I mean, I guess I do check it a bit. Most mornings I end up looking at my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, just to see how the sagging looks, and I usually put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. I have to use this special foundation now, it's supposed to be \"illuminating,\" but honestly it just smells like sunscreen. I bought it at that department store downtown, the one that's closing next month. It’s such a shame they’re closing, they always had the best perfume counters. The ladies there would give you those little free samples in the tiny glass vials. I used to keep them in my purse, but then one broke and my entire bag smelled like lavender and sandalwood for six months. Every time I opened it to get my wallet, it was like walking into a spa. My husband hated it, he said it gave him a headache, but he gets a headache if the TV is too loud, so... \n\nOh, right, fixing my skin. So yeah, I do the makeup, and sometimes when I'm at work I catch myself comparing my face to the other women in the office. Like Linda, she's older than me but her jawline is so sharp. I don't know how she does it. Probably good genes, or maybe she gets something done, she never says. And like I mentioned before, I do try to avoid being in casual pictures, like at book club or whatever, if I’m not feeling okay about how my face looks that day. But I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or touching my face all day long or anything. Just the mirror checking and the makeup in the morning, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your skin causes you to do things repeatedly, like checking mirrors, applying makeup, or comparing your face to others, even if you wish you didn’t have to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't have to do it so much. Like I was saying, most mornings I end up checking my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, and then I put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. It just takes time, you know? Time I could use to actually enjoy my coffee instead of gulping it down. I buy this really nice French roast from a local roaster, it's fair trade and everything. The guy who runs the shop is so sweet, he always remembers my order. He told me he used to be a jazz musician in New Orleans before he moved up here to roast coffee. Can you imagine? Going from playing the saxophone in the French Quarter to bagging beans in the Midwest. He said he just wanted a quieter life. Which I totally get. Sometimes the noise in the city just drives me up the wall, especially when the garbage trucks come clanging down the alley at 5 AM. I've called the city council about it, but they just transfer you from one department to another until you give up. Typical bureaucracy. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, the skin stuff and wishing I didn't do it. Yeah, so the makeup and the checking in the morning, and then like I mentioned, sometimes I compare my face to the women I see at work, like Linda. And I do avoid being in those casual pictures at book club or whatever if I’m not feeling okay about how I look. It would be nice to just not care, to just snap a photo and not worry about the sagging or whatever. So, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't feel like I had to do all that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean... I wouldn't say my skin is the *only* thing I worry about. Not at all. I worry about plenty of things. I mean, work is always a stressor. The attorneys at the firm are constantly pushing deadlines, and if I don't have the briefs formatted exactly right, it's my head on the chopping block. Just last week, Mr. Henderson—he's the senior partner, been there since the earth cooled—he wanted me to track down a deposition from a case in 1998. 1998! We didn't even have everything digitized back then. I spent three hours in the basement archives. It smells like mildew and old paper down there, which, honestly, my husband loves that smell. He buys those candles that are supposed to smell like old books. I don't get it. Why would you want your living room to smell like a dusty library? I prefer something fresh, like lemon or maybe pine. We used to go to this cabin up north every summer when the kids were little, and the pine trees smelled amazing. We'd rent a pontoon boat and just float around all day... 

But, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, worrying about other things. Yeah, I worry about work, and my husband's cholesterol, and whether the roof is going to hold up through another winter. The skin thing is just... it's just something that bothers me when I look in the mirror or when I'm around people like Linda. But it's definitely not the only thing on my mind.

I already had to wash my hair twice just to get the paint smell out... \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Oh, right, the skin. Yeah, I just think it looks flawed, you know? Saggy and uneven. It's just really noticeable to me now with that new lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly, like checking mirrors, touching your face, or trying to fix how your skin looks in response to how you feel about it?\n\nPatient: Well, repeatedly... I mean, I guess I do check it a bit. Most mornings I end up looking at my face from a few different angles in that new mirror, just to see how the sagging looks, and I usually put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse. I have to use this special foundation now, it's supposed to be \"illuminating,\" but honestly it just smells like sunscreen. I bought it at that department store downtown, the one that's closing next month. It’s such a shame they’re closing, they always had the best perfume counters. The ladies there would give you those little free samples in the tiny glass vials. I used to keep them in my purse, but then one broke and my entire bag smelled like lavender and sandalwood for six months. Every time I opened it to get my wallet, it was like walking into a spa. My husband hated it, he said it gave him a headache, but he gets a headache if the TV is too loud, so... \n\nOh, right, fixing my skin. So yeah, I do the makeup, and sometimes when I'm at work I catch myself comparing my face to the other women in the office. Like Linda, she's older than me but her jawline is so sharp. I don't know how she does it. Probably good genes, or maybe she gets something done, she never says. And like I mentioned before, I do try to avoid being in casual pictures, like at book club or whatever, if I’m not feeling okay about how my face looks that day. But I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or touching my face all day long or anything. Just the mirror checking and the makeup in the morning, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your skin causes you to do things repeatedly, like checking mirrors, applying makeup, or comparing your face to others, even if you wish you didn’t have to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't have to do it so much. It just takes time, you know? Time I could use to actually enjoy my coffee instead of gulping it down. I buy this really nice French roast from a local roaster, it's fair trade and everything. The guy who runs the shop is so sweet, he always remembers my order. He told me he used to be a jazz musician in New Orleans before he moved up here to roast coffee. Can you imagine? Going from playing the saxophone in the French Quarter to bagging beans in the Midwest. He said he just wanted a quieter life. Which I totally get. Sometimes the noise in the city just drives me up the wall, especially when the garbage trucks come clanging down the alley at 5 AM. I've called the city council about it, but they just transfer you from one department to another until you give up. Typical bureaucracy. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, the skin stuff and wishing I didn't do it. It would be nice to just not care, to just snap a photo and not worry about the sagging or whatever. So, yeah, I guess I do wish I didn't feel like I had to do all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worries about your skin are the main focus of your concerns, or do you also find yourself frequently worrying about other things, like work, family, or other responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say my skin is the *only* thing I worry about. Not at all. I worry about plenty of things. I mean, work is always a stressor. The attorneys at the firm are constantly pushing deadlines, and if I don't have the briefs formatted exactly right, it's my head on the chopping block. He buys those candles that are supposed to smell like old books. I don't get it. Why would you want your living room to smell like a dusty library? I prefer something fresh, like lemon or maybe pine. We used to go to this cabin up north every summer when the kids were little, and the pine trees smelled amazing. We'd rent a pontoon boat and just float around all day... \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, worrying about other things. But it's definitely not the only thing on my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, no, not really. I don't just feel on edge when things are fine. If things are fine, then they're fine, you know? It's when things actually go wrong that I get stressed. Like yesterday, I was perfectly fine until my car wouldn't start. I drive a Honda Civic, usually it's so reliable, but the battery just completely died. I always hold my breath when I have to pass one. My sister, she actually drove a truck for a few years, long haul, coast to coast. She said the views out West were incredible, like driving through a postcard. She brought me back this beautiful turquoise necklace from New Mexico once. Real silver, too. I don't wear it much though, because it's a bit chunky for my taste, and I feel like it draws attention to my neck... and my skin, obviously. 

But anyway, no, if things are going fine, I'm fine. I don't just sit around feeling anxious for the fun of it.

She has this gorgeous wrap-around porch, too, the kind with the painted wooden floorboards. Her husband built it. He's a carpenter, does really beautiful custom cabinetry mostly. We were actually thinking about having him redo our kitchen cabinets, but honestly, who wants to live through a kitchen remodel? You end up eating microwave meals and takeout for two months. And I just can't stand frozen dinners. The chicken always tastes like rubber, and the mashed potatoes are so watery... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, if people think my worries aren't a big deal. Yeah, no, I think anyone would worry about the things I worry about. It's just normal life stuff, you know? I don't just sit around panicking over little things that don't matter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge about many different things throughout the day, even when things seem to be going fine?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I don't just feel on edge when things are fine. If things are fine, then they're fine, you know? It's when things actually go wrong that I get stressed. Like yesterday, I was perfectly fine until my car wouldn't start. I drive a Honda Civic, usually it's so reliable, but the battery just completely died. I had to call roadside assistance, and the guy who showed up took forever. He said there was traffic on the interstate, which, to be fair, there always is around this time of year with the road construction. They've been working on that same stretch of highway for what feels like a decade. They put up those concrete barriers and the lanes get so narrow, it's terrifying, especially when you're driving next to one of those massive semi-trucks. I always hold my breath when I have to pass one. My sister, she actually drove a truck for a few years, long haul, coast to coast. She said the views out West were incredible, like driving through a postcard. She brought me back this beautiful turquoise necklace from New Mexico once. Real silver, too. I don't wear it much though, because it's a bit chunky for my taste, and I feel like it draws attention to my neck... and my skin, obviously. \n\nBut anyway, no, if things are going fine, I'm fine. I don't just sit around feeling anxious for the fun of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about many different areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or health, even when things seem to be going as expected?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, if things are going as expected, why would I be worried? I'm not the kind of person who just invents problems. If work is fine, it's fine. Although, I will say, things rarely go exactly as expected at the firm. The attorneys are always changing their minds at the last minute. Just last week, Mr. Davis decided he wanted to completely reorganize the filing system for the entire Smithson case. Three hundred boxes of documents, and he wanted them color-coded by date instead of alphabetically by client. I spent three days straight just ordering new folders. I had to go to three different office supply stores because nobody had enough of the specific teal color he wanted. The last store I went to, the one over on 5th Street, they were actually having a massive clearance sale because they're closing down. It's such a shame, they've been there forever. I remember buying my daughter's first set of school supplies there when she started kindergarten. She got this little Lisa Frank backpack with the neon kittens on it. She loved that thing so much she wouldn't even take it off to eat dinner. She's in her second year of college now, studying marine biology. She's hoping to get an internship in Florida next summer to work with manatees. Manatees are so strange looking, aren't they? Like big floating potatoes. But they seem very gentle. \n\nAnyway... what were you asking? Oh, if I worry when things are fine. No, I don't. If there's no problem, I don't worry about it. It's only when there's an actual issue, like my skin, or Mr. Davis changing his mind again, that I get stressed."
